14:T1040,# GOAL F — A first chat turn should not cost a retry cycle

Roughly 30 % of shared-chat streaming sends fail and are retried by the client. Users rarely see an
error; they pay for it in time to first reply.

Parent issue: elizaOS/eliza#22508 · scope: shared runtime + Durable Object.

## Never declare this goal done on the basis of

- the client's retry succeeding — that is what hides this today;
- pointing at `shared-runtime-conversation.ts:1764` and writing "cache warming". True, useless, and
  it leaves the problem untouched;
- absorbing the failure server-side — retrying the DO fetch inside `coordinateSharedStream`, or
  awaiting pending hydration instead of throwing. The client-visible rate drops and the user still
  waits;
- a re-measurement that is within noise. n=61 of 199 gives a 95 % CI of roughly ±6.4 points on a
  30 % rate, so a result of 24 % proves nothing. State a target and a sample size before measuring;
- Worker-side and DO-side counts taken from the observability API and compared to each other — that
  API drops events class-dependently, so the comparison is not sound in either direction.

## Exact result to prove

1. The failing turn no longer fails — not the retry succeeding faster.
2. A measured rate drop with a pre-stated sample size, taken the same way before and after, using
   sliced queries.
3. Time to first reply on a cold open improves, shown as a distribution rather than a median.

## What is actually wrong

Measured over 72 hours on `POST .../messages/stream`:

```
200  n=138  p50=3000  p90=6000  max=10000 ms
503  n=61   p50=6000  p90=9000  max=13000 ms   (30.0 %)
```

The failures cost more than the successes. 87 % recover on the client's automatic retry about six
seconds later, so the user experiences roughly ten seconds to first token. 343 seconds wasted across
61 attempts.

**The 503 is not a mystery, and the earlier framing of it as one was wrong.** It is
`SharedRuntimeCacheWarmingError`, translated at `shared-runtime-conversation.ts:1762-1777` to
`conversation_cache_warming`, then rewritten to `shared_runtime_cache_warming` by
`conversation-coordinator.ts:251-255`. That renaming is why grepping for `warming` in the logs found
nothing and made the branch look unreachable. Do not treat the absence of that string as evidence.

**The source already documents this phenomenon.** `prewarm-shared-agent.ts:1-25` states that a fresh
shared agent's first turn is served entirely from caches, fail-closed, and
`prewarm-shared-agent.ts:81` logs `"[shared-runtime prewarm] leg failed; first turn falls back to
warming 503s"`. That line, and `canonical-scoped-stream.ts:209`, are the diagnostic strings worth
grepping.

**The fix most likely lives in the prewarm/keepwarm rail, which was missing from the earlier
analysis.** `packages/cloud/api/v1/cron/shared-agent-keepwarm/route.ts:48-51` skips exactly the
identities that produce these 503s. Start there.

**The one comparison worth making** is not `/bridge` against `/stream` as populations — those differ
in composition. Split `/bridge` by `rpc.method` and compare `/bridge message.send` against `/stream`.
Those two run the same gates. If that gap is still large, the difference is real and diagnostic; if
it collapses, the earlier "60× gap" was an artifact of mixing methods.

**Cold open is slow for a related but distinct reason.** Across 15 real sessions, cold open to first
reply is p50 18.8 s, max 406 s, with a median of five sequential requests before the first reply and
13 of 15 containing at least one 503. Hibernation is refuted — idle makes it *faster*:
`GET /api/conversations` after >60 min idle is p50 20 ms against 110 ms after <10 s idle. What was
described in the original QA pass as a ~34 s "wake from sleep" is a serial startup burst against a
cold isolate plus a retry cycle. Reducing that burst is client-side sequencing work as much as
server work, and it compounds with GOAL C.

## Definition of done

The three proofs, with the sample size stated in advance and all measurements taken from sliced
queries. Proof 1 in particular: show the first turn succeeding, not the second one arriving sooner.

# Risks

Medium. # Background

## What does this PR do?

- Replaces simulated connector/gateway LARP with executable domain and connector contracts against production seams.
- Adds explicit evidence scopes, fail-closed provider qualification, offline Ed25519 operator authorization, signed evidence verification/rendering, an exact-inventory catalog gate, and 13 provider canaries.
- Adds binding negative-effect, approval-order, idempotency, durable readback, ownership, webhook auth/dedupe, and exact provider-request assertions.
- Repairs all 200 personality scenarios, Gmail contracts, relationship/reminder/cross-platform cases, and corpus quality guards.
- Replaces invented payment actions with real cloud settlement integration tests and hardens amount, currency, provider, expiry, replay, and callback behavior.
- Adds production `GOOGLE_WORKSPACE` Drive/Sheets actions and confirmation-gated exact read/write contracts.
- Removes stale aliases/claims, records scenario retirements, classifies every maintained scenario explicitly, and rejects future weak/deceptive patterns.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes and improvements, with additive provider-qualification and Google Workspace action surfaces.

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation was updated for evidence scopes, provider qualification, honest simulated-contract labeling, pending-vs-retired policy, and current scenario paths.

# Testing

# Risks

**Low, with one risk claim corrected after review.** The diff is the regenerated data file plus one generator function. (1) *Dropped live actions* — ruled out: every sampled removed key traces to a real rename or deletion (receipts below), and the enumerating audit walks all four scan roots including `plugins/`. (2) *Lost curated terms* — **this PR's original claim here was wrong, and ss251's review proved it**: seeding from `existing.base` was not preservation, because `limitTerms` re-scores at the 96-term cap (multi-word +5) and the description-extract flood evicted committed short tokens (`web` from `action.browser.request`; `skill`, `skills`, `install`, `enable`, `search`, `registry` from `action.skill.request`) — exactly the word-boundary tokens `textIncludesKeywordTerm` matches on. Fixed structurally: `limitTermsPinned` keeps every committed `existing.base` term verbatim and fills only the remaining slots by score; verified over all 114 committed entries — zero surviving keys lose any committed base term. (3) *Churn without convergence* — a second generate+format run is still byte-identical, and no source or test anywhere in `packages/` or `plugins/` references any added or removed key.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Regenerates `packages/shared/src/i18n/keywords/action-search.generated.keywords.json`, and — responding to ss251's review — adds `limitTermsPinned` to `packages/scripts/generate-action-search-keywords.mjs` so committed curated terms are structurally non-evictable. This artifact feeds a real runtime path: `packages/shared/scripts/generate-keywords.mjs` compiles `keywords/*.keywords.json` into the `validation-keyword-data` modules imported by `src/i18n/keyword-matching.ts` in both `@elizaos/shared` and `@elizaos/core`. Against today's surface (`audit-action-availability.mjs`: 160 actions → 140 stems):

| | count |
|---|---|
| entries before → after | 114 → 140 |
| added — current actions with **no keywords today** | 106 |
| removed — actions that no longer exist | 80 |
| modified (additive-only for curated terms, post-pin) | 26 |
| identical | 8 |

Removal receipts (spot-verified): `action.bash.request` → the action is now `name: "SHELL"` (`plugins/plugin-coding-tools/src/actions/bash.ts:1148`, `"BASH"` demoted to a simile; `action.shell.request` is in the added set). All 10 `action.linear*.request` keys → `plugins/plugin-linear` deleted in dc03207dc. `action.googleCalendar.request` → the live action is `CALENDAR` (`action.calendar.request` added).

Until this lands, the 106 unkeyed actions fall back to generic locale terms in multilingual action search instead of their names, similes, and context phrases.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (stale generated data resynchronized with its source of truth).

# Documentation changes needed?

None; the generator's header already documents the artifact's role.

# Summary

Unifies Eliza onboarding around a short, anticipatory, useful-first assistant across app, Shared, dedicated workspace, messaging, connector, and voice paths.

- Makes the canonical character concise and action-oriented without mechanically ending every turn with a question.
- Gives Shared a detailed typed capability catalog and an inline personal-workspace handoff that preserves the exact request for review; consequential work is never auto-executed.
- Adds progressive owner-profile acquisition for preferred name, stable location, and timezone, with provenance/confidence precedence, correction/deletion, Unicode support, and a real Shared-runtime provider/evaluator. Phone and IP are not used to infer location.
- Preserves first-turn free text through setup and replaces generic launch prompts with a concrete likely action.
- Adds contained connector authorization, connected acknowledgements, durable account-link/workspace-ready/subscription-upgraded/connector-connected follow-ups, and authenticated in-app claim/ack replay.
- Adds a signed caller-to-self SMS handoff during Twilio voice calls, with E.164 validation, durable CallSid idempotency, a stable provider idempotency token, history repair, and truthful spoken receipts.
- Generalizes durable post-link continuation to Discord, Telegram, Blooio, and Twilio without silently opting web/app users into cross-channel messages.

# Safety and privacy

- Handoff, lifecycle, connector, profile, and channel-hint payloads are parsed and bounded at trust boundaries.
- Continuations are tenant, user, agent, and provider bound; they expire and are offer-only.
- Explicitly forgotten owner facts suppress lower-confidence hints until the owner supplies a replacement.
- Web/app lifecycle events stay in-app unless the user explicitly selected a verified messaging origin or preference.
- Voice SMS can target only the HMAC-attested caller number, never an arbitrary recipient; ambiguous provider outcomes cannot authorize a resend.
- Upgrade races converge on one target; lifecycle enqueue precedes exact continuation cleanup so outages retain retry authority without clearing a newer request.

18:T16fd,Closes #22399

## Summary

- add migration 0266 for tenant-bound GET, HEAD, LIST, and presign authority, immutable response receipts, exact ledger settlement, and stable renewal lineage
- settle provider success before the one atomic debit; persist terminal zero-cost and insufficient-credit outcomes for exact replay
- mint opaque, host/method/expiry-bound native read capabilities with rotation support and no tenant, logical key, or provider key in URLs
- move storage identifiers and LIST prefixes to typed authenticated headers on fixed SDK routes
- preserve exact provider generations across replay, revocation, DELETE/GC races, expired settlement fences, and presign renewals
- update Discord voice storage to use the fixed route/header contract without logging capability or logical-key material

## Independent-review repairs

- every storage-read receipt now rejects row DELETE and table TRUNCATE, so an attached ledger debit cannot be made deletable by first removing its receipt
- birth is strictly `prepared` with no response, provider/result, provider-success/completion, debit, revocation, or access state; request-owned `object_id`, capability fields, retention, and presign renewal lineage remain valid
- prepared and provider-succeeded operations cannot hold a debit; a debit may attach only during a paid `provider_succeeded` to `committed` transition
- prepared-state CHECKs prevent direct same-state result/response prepopulation, while provider-succeeded rows cannot prepopulate completion or access state
- migration adversaries cover DELETE/TRUNCATE then credit deletion, prepared and provider-succeeded debit attachment, every forbidden birth field, and valid presign root/renewal prepared shapes

## Safety invariants

- organization, idempotency digest, server price, object generation, provider outcome, debit, and response receipt remain one tenant-scoped authority chain
- concurrent duplicates serialize into one operation; response/ACK loss replays the committed receipt without another debit
- provider failure or integrity mismatch cannot debit; insufficient credit cannot disclose a successful provider response
- DELETE and generation GC cannot collect an object while a durable read receipt retains it
- capability signing fails closed when secrets are missing or undersized and supports verification-only previous rotation secrets
- SDK and gateway callers never place logical identifiers or sensitive capability material in URLs or logs

## Summary
- settle delayed agent and container compute intervals with immutable segmented rate history and exact tenant-bound ledger receipts
- make insufficient-funding stop decisions, provider retries, terminal attention, and provider confirmation durable for both workload kinds

- preserve unpaid debt cursors on failure; permit policy-scoped container earnings conversion without caller-controlled rates

## Independent-review repairs
- separate `user_request` suspension from `billing_request`; user authority reuses manual work but creates an independent follow-up when billing is pending or in progress; suspend executions serialize on the workload row
- active-billing cancellation is unconditional `user_request`, so funded cancellations cannot be canceled by billing revalidation
- migration safely stamps exact bound legacy suspend jobs as billing; runtime also derives missing authority only from the exact active tenant/agent/job intent binding
- billing stop revalidation settles the full exact accrued debt; a microscopic positive balance cannot cancel shutdown or forgive the cursor
- accept only exact Docker `No such container/object` evidence for the expected runtime after a crash; generic not-found remains retryable
- missing, unsupported, or malformed Hetzner metadata fails closed and cannot fabricate provider confirmation

- provider I/O occurs only after the current workload row and lifecycle generation are rechecked under the workload -> organization -> earnings lock order
- provider failure never marks a live workload stopped or removes it from independent recovery scans
- restart/reactivation cannot forgive elapsed debt: exact settlement, debit, receipt, generation advance, and intent supersession commit together or roll back together
- receipt identity and ledger linkage are tenant scoped and immutable

1a:T11c0,## Summary

This draft is the independently mergeable native-R2 mutation and catalog foundation for #21045 and #22327. It replaces split-brain authenticated mutations with one catalog-backed `env.BLOB` authority while deliberately excluding incomplete paid reads.

- PUT writes deterministic immutable generation keys, reserves `max(new-old, 0)` quota, binds authoritative server pricing into the durable request digest, and publishes the catalog pointer only when the exact tenant-bound credit hold settles in the same primary transaction.
- Legacy `org/<tenant>/<key>` objects are discovered with cursor-complete R2 LIST, adopted with already-counted quota, and transferred exactly on first native overwrite.
- GET and HEAD resolve the committed catalog generation so newly written objects remain readable, but are explicitly unbilled until durable paid-read receipts exist.
- LIST is catalog-backed and explicitly unbilled; non-recursive filtering occurs before the SQL limit.
- Presign authenticates and then returns a fixed 503 without parsing the key or initializing catalog, provider, signing, pricing, or billing authority.
- DELETE is a zero-price durable operation under the current server pricing policy. It retains pointer/quota until R2 DELETE plus strongly consistent HEAD absence, then atomically tombstones and releases quota.
- Reconciliation uses fenced leases/CAS across prepared, reserved, provider-started, and provider-absence quarantine states. Superseded generations remain pinned for 24 hours before GC.
- Credit links are tenant-bound and credit transaction tenant provenance is immutable. Pricing precision is widened to `numeric(18,12)` and an audited migration reseeds a historical zero `storage.put_per_byte` row to `0.000000001`.

Cloudflare's synchronous strongly consistent mutation/HEAD guarantees support immutable generations plus strong-HEAD reconciliation: https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/api/workers/workers-api-reference/ and https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/reference/consistency/

## Safety invariants

- No caller-controlled price reaches mutation settlement.
- Decimal price legs are summed exactly and rounded once to six ledger decimals.
- Debit, settlement, quota publication, catalog publication, and terminal PUT receipt are one authoritative transaction boundary.
- Refund, quota release, and terminal failure receipt are atomic and lease-fenced.
- Negative provider HEAD is quarantined and rechecked; a late writer that lost its lease deletes its immutable generation.
- Historical quota is recomputed from the complete catalog plus active reservations before first native access.
- Provider keys, logical keys, idempotency keys, and signed URLs are excluded from application logs.
- A tombstone prevents legacy resurrection.
- No incomplete paid GET, HEAD, LIST, or presign path can run from this PR.

## Exact-head evidence

Head: `faff8dbe0a7c65ec81e25c2ab5ed9f3995dda290`
Base: `b2d03f8aca5e6b5902c4b5f9bcf86f61c3f3bebb`

- Native service failure injection: 13/13 tests, 45 assertions, 100% function/line coverage.
- PGlite repository/concurrency: 17/17 tests, 51 assertions, including real atomic debit/refund, cross-tenant rejection, concurrent admission/recovery, stale lease fencing, quota transfer, and terminal replay.
- Active CRUD: 5/5, proving PUT→unbilled GET/HEAD→overwrite→zero-cost durable DELETE.
- Native LIST: 2/2, proving catalog reconciliation without pricing/credit calls.
- Presign gate: 1/1, proving authenticated fixed denial before request-body parsing.
- Real Workerd/Miniflare R2: 1/1, proving conditional immutable writes and strong GET/HEAD/delete visibility.

1b:T198c,Closes #22401

## What changed

- adds a dedicated durable `stripe_checkout_orders` authority with tenant-scoped client idempotency, immutable request digests, exact organization/user/customer/session/PaymentIntent bindings, and server-owned charge/grant amounts
- enforces USD credit top-ups and validates configured Stripe Price amount/currency against the active credit-pack catalog before creating Checkout
- recovers provider-success/ACK-loss through either a signed webhook or authenticated verification: a trusted receipt must match both order references, tenant/user where authenticated, paid state, exact amount/currency/customer/PaymentIntent, then the Session is CAS-bound and settled transactionally
- creates Stripe Customers under an organization-scoped provider idempotency key and transactionally pins the winning customer to the organization and order before any Session call; concurrent/replayed requests always use the order's immutable winner
- unifies webhook and landing verification through one transactional settlement service; webhook-first, verify-first, concurrent, and replayed settlement converge on one ledger grant while durable projections/invoices remain repairable
- migrates both legacy `/stripe/create-checkout-session` and `/v1/credits/checkout` plus both verification routes to the authority
- quarantines pre-deploy legacy credit-pack Sessions for operator reconciliation instead of deriving entitlement from a mutable/deleted catalog; exact provider receipt and claimed historical identifiers are retained without granting
- serializes cumulative refund/dispute clawback target calculation, idempotency insert, and balance mutation under the organization row lock; duplicate, out-of-order, concurrent, prorated, shortfall, and reinstatement paths are covered
- handles zero-cost idempotency and exact numeric/max-credit bounds without trusting caller pricing metadata

## Migration

`0261_stripe_checkout_orders.sql` follows merged `0260_domain_purchase_authority` contiguously in the journal. Every ledger binding uses NULL-safe `IS DISTINCT FROM`, including PaymentIntent and metadata order linkage. The migration also adds the durable legacy-pack quarantine table; its historical pack identifier deliberately has no catalog foreign key so deleted packs remain reconcilable.

This repository has SQL/journal migrations through 0260 while Drizzle snapshots stop at 0081, so 0261 is a narrowly targeted manual migration with no snapshot, matching the established post-0081 convention. PGlite migration tests cover shape, replay, partial-apply collision, NULL-binding attacks, exact binding, and cross-tenant rejection.

## Consolidated independent-review blockers repaired

1. NULL-safe ledger/order binding in 0261 with adversarial PGlite coverage.

Base: `d09096da34046117c0ae950ee1a0b406e5ccc804`

The focused suites are intentionally invoked one file per Bun process because repository test files replace whole auth modules with stateful `mock.module` fixtures; this avoids cross-file mock contamination while exercising every affected contract.

## Legacy tenant-binding review delta

Legacy cutover now locks and verifies `users.organization_id` against the provider-linked organization before any custom grant or pack quarantine. The quarantine table uses a replay-safe trigger that selects the user `FOR SHARE`, compares with `IS DISTINCT FROM`, and freezes the organization/user tuple; this avoids a blocking composite unique-index build on the live users table. PGlite proves cross-tenant custom settlement leaves zero credits/ledger and the queue emits zero referral/invoice effects, cross-tenant packs leave no quarantine row, valid same-org custom settlement succeeds, valid packs quarantine, and trigger replay/immutability hold.

## Exact quarantine replay and lock-race delta

Quarantine creation now uses `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`; a conflict locks and loads the existing Session row and accepts only an exact canonical match across PaymentIntent, tenant, user, customer, pack, claimed credits, amount, currency, reason, and the full provider receipt. Any collision or changed replay fails closed. The database trigger freezes all authority columns and permits only `updated_at` and explicit operator `resolved_at` changes. Its INSERT membership lookup uses `FOR SHARE`, which blocks all user-row updates and deletes.

A new opt-in real-PostgreSQL two-session integration test proves both lock orders via `pg_blocking_pids`: quarantine insertion blocks organization reassignment until commit, while reassignment-first makes a stale-tenant quarantine block and then reject without a row. The test typechecks and is included in the repository lane; it skipped locally because no responsive Docker/PostgreSQL harness was available. PGlite additionally covers exact replay plus changed PaymentIntent, tenant, amount, provider receipt, and direct authority mutations.

# Risks

Medium. This is a broad but coherent boundary-hardening consolidation across
filesystem, parser, archive, subprocess, network-decode, local-trust, and MCP
schema-validation paths. The main compatibility risks are stricter rejection of
previously accepted hostile/ambiguous inputs and owner-only host-file imports.
The affected public behavior is pinned by package tests and the repository-wide
type/lint/audit gate.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

It retains the useful findings from the ss251 PR wave while correcting issues
that made the individual heads unsafe to merge directly:

- Bounds file/media reads and writes before allocation, preserves a single
  opened-file identity through mutation, and treats replacement text literally.
- Validates PDF/PNG/GBNF/ZIP structure and expansion budgets before expensive
  parser or decompression work, including local/central ZIP metadata agreement.
- Makes Smithers, ffmpeg/ffprobe, Matrix sync, Anthropic CLI, VFS regex, and MCP
  schema execution deadlines authoritative even when teardown or evaluation
  stalls.
- Isolates hostile MCP JSON Schema compile/evaluation in resource-limited
  workers with a four-worker ceiling and bounded schema/argument serialization.
- Centralizes first-party CORS and loopback request trust, binds browser
  Origin/Referer authority to Host (including ports and IPv6), rejects proxy and
  same-site ambiguity, and preserves native-app origins.
- Uses canonical request-body and decode boundaries, typed errors, and explicit
  authorization instead of denylists, test-only exports, or broad catches.
- Parses shell syntax with the existing quote-aware analyzer before compatibility
  execution and limits arbitrary host document imports to owner/agent-self paths.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes and security hardening; no intended breaking public API change.

# Documentation changes needed?

No user documentation change is required. Existing package contracts remain
the authority; this change hardens their failure boundaries.

1f:Td71,## Summary

- settle crypto payment status, organization credit, app-purchase effects, invoice projection, referral earnings, and callback intent in one caller-owned database transaction
- bind OxaPay settlement to the server-stored provider track ID, order ID, fiat amount, and fiat currency; accept only USD credit purchases
- make confirmed replay re-prove the canonical settlement and reconstruct missing invoice/callback projections without minting again
- persist immutable app-charge callback envelopes and deterministic room-message identities, with retry leases and independent room/HTTP checkpoints
- prepare and sign direct-wallet sweep transactions before provider submission, persist the exact bytes/hash, and retry only those same bytes after acknowledgement loss
- establish one transaction-hash identity across writers: EVM hashes canonicalize to lowercase, Solana identifiers retain case, and migration 0255 fails closed on active legacy collisions

## Safety properties

- failures after app writes, credits, invoice creation, referral writes, callback enqueue, or sweep enqueue roll back every database settlement leg
- concurrent webhook/manual delivery converges on one settlement; cross-tenant EVM casing aliases cannot double-spend
- provider failure/expiry status and the failed app callback intent commit atomically; a later paid state cannot be overwritten by failure
- callback retry does not repeat an already-delivered channel and reuses an immutable payload/deterministic room identity
- sweep acknowledgement loss cannot create a fresh transfer: EVM and Solana retry the persisted signed transaction only
- OxaPay settlement rebinds track/order/amount/currency to the server quote; caller-selected callback endpoints and non-USD fiat are rejected

Independent review found and fixed duplicate-JSON-key ambiguity at the owner review boundary: JSON rules and decisions now reject duplicate decoded keys, including escaped equivalents, so a visually reviewed `keep` cannot execute as a later duplicate `delete`.21:T1d81,Closes #22368

## Summary

- replace the deletable 24-hour domain-buy claim with a durable tenant-scoped state machine
- pin the authoritative USD quote before a keyed ledger debit
- persist the registry-minimum registration term in the attempt's first write, before quote construction, so a crash or concurrent loser cannot re-fetch a changed provider minimum
- query the extension registration schema, pin its minimum years in the digest/quote, charge the exact first-year plus renewal-years total, and POST the same term
- persist a registrar lease before the billable Cloudflare POST; acknowledgement loss reconciles by domain instead of repeating or refunding speculatively
- key and SQL-bind debit/refund transactions to the exact organization, domain, amount, and purchase attempt
- recover provider-confirmed failure with one durable refund, and recover successful registrations through idempotent local persistence/assignment
- replay terminal responses exactly and reject request/binding mismatches
- add a scheduled reconciler for charged, ambiguous-provider, registered, and refund-pending attempts
- quarantine expired pre-`0260` attempts when a legacy unrefunded debit exists, preventing a second debit or registrar POST until an operator reconciles the old purchase
- retain safe legacy orphan recovery only for the tenant with an unrefunded purchase proof

## Architecture and provider contract

Cloudflare documents domain name as the registration resource's natural idempotency key. Its registration POST is billable and successful registrations are non-refundable, while registration status is queryable separately. Cloudflare also documents that `years` defaults to the extension's registry minimum (two years for some extensions), with `registration_cost` applying to year one and `renewal_cost` to later years. Accordingly this patch reads the authoritative extension schema before charging, durably pins the term and full cost, sends the exact term on POST, records `provider_started` before POST, and treats transport errors as ambiguous: it polls status and refunds only an authoritative `failed` or `expired` result.

No registrar call, domain purchase, migration, deployment, or cloud mutation was performed while validating this PR.

## Adversarial invariants reviewed

- concurrent create and provider-lease claims have one winner
- an unquoted processing row returns in-progress while its lease is live and resumes from the same immutable term after expiry
- a create-race loser that observes the winner before quote construction performs no debit or registrar POST; provider-minimum drift fails closed against the winner's digest/term
- a debit crash before attempt attachment reuses the keyed committed ledger transaction
- a provider acknowledgement-loss crash never repeats registration or guesses a refund
- a two-year registry minimum is priced as registration plus one renewal year, pinned across retries, and POSTed as two years
- refund retries reuse the exact keyed refund
- charge/refund tenant, type, amount, idempotency key, domain, and attempt bindings are NULL-safe and database-enforced
- the completed managed-domain row must match both the attempt tenant and exact domain
- a different organization cannot recover or assign a registrar orphan
- a completed purchase can be reassigned within its owning organization without a second debit
- a pre-`0260` completed purchase can be reassigned to a second same-org app with no debit, refund, availability check, or registration POST
- an expired legacy row with no `charge_id` is not assumed uncharged: any matching unrefunded debit forces explicit reconciliation instead of deletion/retry
- expired charged/provider/refund/registered work remains discoverable by the cron
- migration `0260` follows the merged `0259` chain and passes Drizzle consistency

22:T1326,## Summary

- Add a first-class computer-use session manager for host, browser, sandbox, and remote-guest targets.
- Enforce one renewable physical-input lease while allowing concurrent isolated targets with virtual cursors.
- Serialize actions with optimistic sequence checks, duplicate-action protection, bounded sanitized events, and safe viewer URLs.
- Expose authenticated session CRUD, action, lease, SSE, and read-only frame routes.
- Add a responsive session monitor and always-on-top desktop floating-window path.

Closes #22363

## Security and concurrency invariants

- Host ownership remains held until in-flight action/frame work settles, including lease expiry and release races.
- Distinct isolated targets may run concurrently; the same target has exclusive ownership.
- Adapter errors are redacted from shared snapshots/events while the immediate request retains an actionable failure.
- External viewers require HTTPS; HTTP is limited to IPv4, hostname, or bracketed IPv6 loopback.
- Untrusted route fields are validated; action parameters and frame bytes are excluded from bounded events.

# Summary

This consolidates evidence review onto one verified run bundle. Matrix runs now
capture a pre-lane baseline, ingest only exact-run producer deltas, finalize one
manifest, verify it, and hand that exact bundle to the reviewer. The legacy
`scripts/view-audit` crawler and the reviewer's implicit raw-silo scan path are
removed.

The integrity boundary is fail closed: source files are captured through stable
no-follow descriptors, hardlink/symlink aliases and physical path overlaps are
rejected, canonical bundles are copy-only and immutable, envelope publication
is exclusive/atomic, certification output cannot replace bundle contents, and
the reviewer consumes private hash-checked snapshots rather than mutable source
paths. Explicit compatibility sources remain supported without becoming the
normal matrix path.

## What changed

- make Cloud-only macOS first launch use a recoverable full onboarding window and suppress the global Fn-key monitor/Input Monitoring prompt
- hide developer viewer apps plus model/provider selection and serving-provider indicators in Cloud-locked UI
- keep Cloud voice/runtime copy truthful without bootstrapping model configuration
- suppress unsupported protected agent probes and wait for a usable managed-Cloud conversation/status boundary before advancing startup
- repair the cross-host desktop SSO/PKCE handoff and external Cloud API-base classification
- allow the direct macOS release stager to accept an intentionally runtime-free Cloud-only bundle while still requiring the embedded runtime for normal builds

## Root causes

The desktop shell and Settings reused local-runtime assumptions in the Cloud-only product. Startup could race unsupported protected probes, the external auth bridge could lose its verifier across hosts, and the direct-release stager unconditionally required `eliza-dist` even when the packaged brand contract declared `cloudOnly: true`.

## User impact

A first-time nontechnical Cloud customer gets a full sign-in-first experience without local-model choices, developer tooling, or a global-keystroke permission prompt. After sign-in, startup waits for a Cloud agent boundary that can actually serve chat. Cloud-only canary packaging now reaches `.app`, updater archive, update manifest, and DMG staging.

# Risks

Large runtime-path change. It affects Personal Shared text, voice, Discord, Telegram, and BlueBubbles/iMessage routing. Main risks are workerd compatibility, first-turn cold hydration, provider latency, conversation idempotency, and transport cancellation. The implementation fails closed on unsupported tools and preserves Durable Object serialization.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Replaces the alternate Shared-agent inference engine with the canonical elizaOS `AgentRuntime` for every ordinary Shared turn.
- Projects the full behavioral character while excluding secrets, settings, knowledge, and plugin authority.
- Adds the `SHARED_RUNTIME_CAPABILITIES` provider and `REQUEST_DEDICATED_UPGRADE` action. The action creates a review handoff only; it cannot provision or charge.
- Loads the edge-compatible web search, reminders, todos, and injected-media capabilities through genuine runtime plugins; Node/container-only capabilities remain explicit Dedicated upgrades.
- Preserves trusted channel provenance (`DM`, `GROUP`, `VOICE_DM`, `VOICE_GROUP`) so the runtime owns should-respond behavior and silent turns.
- Adds content-free timing receipts for runtime initialization, history hydration, compose state, provider totals/slowest provider, routing, first model text/audio, TTS transport, and upstream retries.
- Reuses the Cartesia transport across the opening greeting and first response, makes barge-in cancel only active synthesis, and wakes cold-turn retries as soon as prewarm completes.
- Integrates the canonical workerd schema-convergence fix from #22582 via the now-merged upstream commit.
- Repairs integration-base test fixtures exposed by the full repository gate.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature, performance improvement, and regression fix; no intended public API break.

# Documentation changes needed?

No end-user documentation change is required. The capability provider is the runtime-visible documentation of Shared versus Dedicated support.

## Summary

- add the versioned provider-neutral connected-account and capability DTOs removed by #21696, without restoring the unsafe v1 authorization model
- bind policy to an immutable selected-account snapshot, capability, operation, contextual risk, provider-owned exact-input digest, and binding time
- make both allowed and confirmation-required decisions consume-once through one trusted atomic authorization boundary
- bind the complete policy and confirmation grant digests into the in-process dispatch authority and canonical effect receipt
- keep successful, designed-empty, unavailable, and error outcomes structurally distinct without exposing provider payloads or credentials

## Security contract

- Public account IDs are opaque handles; provider credentials, request payloads, and secret material are rejected from the wire contracts.
- Contextual risk may be elevated but never downgraded below the selected account capability catalog.
- The request digest includes the selected account/provider/mode, catalog risk, requested capability/risk, operation, exact-input digest, request identity, and binding time.
- `CapabilityAuthorizationConsumer.consume` is the single trusted host boundary. Durable hosts must atomically compare-and-delete the current account/capability policy decision and its exact confirmation grant; a separate verify/delete sequence is replayable.
- Allowed decisions are one-shot too. Lack of interactive confirmation does not create reusable dispatch authority.
- The returned authority is branded, recursively immutable, and intentionally not a wire credential.
- Receipts bind the exact authorization, complete policy digest, optional confirmation digest, request/account/capability/operation/input identities, canonical `EffectReceipt`, and post-authorization chronology.

29:Tbba,Fixes #22478.

## Outcome

Makes the first terminal app-chat reservation settlement a durable database authority. A stale sweep that wins before a late provider result, or a provider result that wins before the sweep, now replays the same terminal receipt exactly once instead of retrying a mismatched generic credit idempotency key.

## Design

- Adds migration `0268_app_reservation_settlement_authority` and the immutable `app_reservation_settlements` receipt, snapshotting tenant, app, user, creator, exact six-decimal economics, outcome, source, and committed projection IDs.
- Claims the winner and applies organization refund/overage, creator earnings, app counters, receipt, and `settled_at` in one transaction under reservation -> organization -> creator -> app lock order.
- Replays the stored receipt for duplicate or opposite-order delivery; generic credit idempotency remains strict and mismatched amounts are never accepted.
- Backfills only uniquely reconstructable pre-cutover settlements. Irreconstructable historical terminal rows receive a validated, non-sensitive quarantine authority and replay as a fail-closed terminal no-op without fabricated economics.
- Freezes receipt-backed projection fields. Direct projection deletion/truncation is rejected while existing user/app owner cascades remain valid and retain the immutable receipt evidence.
- Validates tenant and normalized UUID identity at the database and runtime boundaries.

## Exact-head evidence

Head: `9d6bd01593c6238baa8118b3ce12cce494edc90d`

- PGlite migration/cutover/adversarial guard suite: 5 passed, 32 assertions, including receipt-after-quarantine rejection with the single quarantine unchanged.
- App reservation authority/sweep suite: 12 passed, 95 assertions.
- Credit idempotency suite: 19 passed, 96 assertions.
- Hold concurrency suite: 11 passed, 102 assertions.
- Legacy reconciliation suite: 8 passed, 52 assertions.
- Real PostgreSQL migration/trigger/cascade suite: 2 passed, 18 assertions. Both lock orders inspect `pg_blocking_pids` before releasing the winner and prove exactly one terminal authority: receipt-first leaves receipt 1/quarantine 0; quarantine-first rejects the waiting receipt and leaves receipt 0/quarantine 1.
- `drizzle-kit check`: passed.
- Biome on all changed maintained files: passed (12 files).
- `git diff --check origin/develop...HEAD`: passed.
- Scoped shared typecheck reached only existing workspace dependency failures in `plugin-elizacloud` (`@clack/prompts`, `viem/accounts`, `js-tiktoken`, `jose`, `undici`, and `ai`); no changed settlement file emitted a diagnostic.

# What this PR does

- Replaces process-memory fulfillment authority for v1 Stripe/OxaPay webhooks with one database transaction covering provider-event persistence, exact ledger credit, payment-request settlement, and durable callback intent.
- Requires a server-owned USD quote and exact provider amount/currency. Non-USD top-ups and unsupported payer claims fail before provider state is created.
- Uses the real Stripe PaymentIntent as the Stripe credit key, preserving existing refund/dispute clawback authority. OxaPay uses a provider-qualified request key.
- Binds every replay to tenant, request, provider, provider transaction, amount, currency, and semantic verified settlement identity.
- Treats an OxaPay callback only as a signed signal, then obtains authoritative paid identity/amount/currency from Payment Information inquiry. Caller metadata cannot redirect the provider settlement callback.
- Accepts exact equivalent OxaPay USD decimals with trailing zeros and rejects non-zero sub-cent amounts.
- Prevents settled requests from regressing; late failure callbacks are superseded. Distinct Stripe delivery IDs for the same PaymentIntent reuse the original durable callback.
- Defers credit-cache invalidation until after the outer settlement transaction commits.
- Adds a lease-based callback dispatcher with immediate attempt plus cron retry, SSRF-safe delivery, bounded attempts, and transactional supersession.

# Risk

High-value billing path, medium implementation risk. The migration is append-only `0254`. Risk is bounded by row locks, database uniqueness/constraints, exact Decimal comparisons, authoritative quote/provider binding, rollback injection, concurrency tests, and independent exact-head review.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds an AST/TypeScript-resolution audit for whole-module `mock.module` factories. It finds named exports bound by the module graph loaded by each test, compares them with the mock factory, accepts real-module spreads, requires reasoned explicit suppressions, and rejects stale suppressions. A counted baseline exposes and ratchets 105 existing findings while failing new or increased debt.

The command is wired into root `verify`.

## What kind of change is this?

Testing infrastructure improvement.

# Risks

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Requires a nonempty, unique set of positive Hetzner firewall IDs; attaches that exact set atomically in the create request; reads applied attachment state; and rejects nodes whose provider ID, authoritative labels, firewall membership, or attachment state drift. It also removes same-name-only adoption and documents the operator contract.

## What kind of change is this?

Security hardening bug fix.

# Documentation changes needed?

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebasing onto `origin/develop` produced zero conflicts; base at evidence capture: `492bc62904f0641a2598c7cf6bcff7de46539c29`.
- [x] `bun run verify` was attempted; the exact unrelated blocker is documented below.

# Risks

Medium. Browse and by-entity keep their existing exact `total` contract, so a sparse post-filter may scan a large table to exhaustion. Each adapter query is bounded to at most 5,000 rows and advances a contiguous offset; rows are never re-read within a request. Single-token keyword, room, time, table, and agent predicates are pushed into the adapter. This trades bounded per-query memory for correctness without reviving the rejected fixed total-scan cap.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Replaces one-shot over-fetch with per-table contiguous offset windows that grow from 200 rows to a maximum 5,000-row window.
- Pushes `roomId`, `end`, and safe single-token `textContains` predicates into `getMemories`; multi-token queries retain the established OR semantics through post-filtering.
- Drains browse and by-entity tables to true exhaustion so `total` remains exact and every page stays reachable.
- Lets feed stop after `limit + 1` eligible rows per table, preserving an honest `hasMore` without scanning the rest unnecessarily.
- Clones returned memories before tagging their source table, avoiding mutation of adapter-owned objects.
- Adds adversarial route tests for sparse search, entity filtering, empty-content runs, deep offset 20,000, exact exhaustion, predicate pushdown, contiguous work, and multi-table ordering.

The design lineage is credited to Uuriko's scoped issue claim, which identified contiguous per-table windows, supported predicate pushdown, the SQL adapter's entity-filter boundary, and the need to avoid #22066's cap/prefix-reread regression. This implementation preserves those constraints and chooses exact browse totals rather than introducing an incomplete-total UI contract.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No. The public response shape remains unchanged and the implementation restores its documented pagination semantics.

The exact-head route handler was invoked through `GET /api/memories/browse?type=messages&q=needle%20missing&limit=25&offset=25` over a deterministic 1,000-row adapter-shaped store where every tenth row matches. The multi-token OR query cannot safely push a single text predicate, so the route scanned each source row once in contiguous windows and returned the correct second page plus exact total:

```json
{
  "response": {
    "count": 25,
    "total": 100,
    "limit": 25,
    "offset": 25,
    "first": "00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000250",
    "last": "00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000490"
  },
  "adapterWindows": [
    { "tableName": "messages", "offset": 0, "limit": 200, "textContains": null },
    { "tableName": "messages", "offset": 200, "limit": 400, "textContains": null },
    { "tableName": "messages", "offset": 600, "limit": 800, "textContains": null }
  ]
}
```

# Risks

Low. The change adds a migration and an atomic write to a money-settlement path.
The main risks are migration compatibility and rejecting legitimate retries;
PGlite integration coverage exercises migration application, concurrent replay,
rollback, provider parity, and tenant isolation.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds a provider-neutral `payment_request_receipts` projection for settled Stripe
and OxaPay payment requests. Each row is bound to its organization, payment
request, provider event, and provider transaction; preserves exact settled cents,
currency, time, payload digest, and provider proof; and is inserted in the same
transaction as credit fulfillment. Exact retries reuse the row while conflicting
settlement metadata aborts the transaction.

The projection is explicitly typed as a `provider_payment_receipt`. It does not
claim tax or legal invoice semantics and does not fabricate Stripe identifiers in
the legacy `invoices` table.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change that completes provider-neutral receipt coverage for
the unified payment-request settlement flow).

# Documentation changes needed?

No project documentation change is required. The schema, migration, and service
headers document the provider-receipt boundary and its separation from tax/legal
invoices.

# Risks

Medium. This changes warm-pool claim/count eligibility and daemon reconciliation ordering. The safety bias is deliberate: an unknown or known-stale digest is fenced from claims immediately, even if that temporarily forces cold provisioning. Physical teardown remains burst/floor bounded. `WARM_POOL_ENABLED` is unchanged and production was not activated.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- resolves one immutable image digest per daemon sweep, pre-pulls and replenishes the exact `repo@sha256` reference, and passes the same digest into rollout;
- makes persisted canonical `image_digest` part of the shared claim/count predicates, so null/malformed and daemon-known stale generations cannot transfer to a user;
- adds exact-generation CAS fencing over status, environment revision, runtime locators, configured image, persisted digest, and readiness;
- fences every stale generation immediately while selecting only a policy-bounded subset for physical teardown, preserving retained depth until target-ready generations exist;
- reports rollout state from persisted digests rather than mutable tag text;
- wires rollout into the real provisioning daemon after a successful immutable pre-pull and before digest-scoped replenish.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix / production safety improvement.

# Documentation changes needed?

N/A - no public contract or operator flag changed; protected rollout remains disabled and tracked by #16961.

Start with `agent-sandboxes.ts` for the claim predicate and generation CAS, then `agent-warm-pool.ts` for fence-versus-teardown policy, then the daemon one-shot test for phase/digest binding.

# Independent exact-head repair review

The first submitted head was not safe to merge. Independent review found and repaired these concrete defects before this final head:

- a zero-node pre-pull (`attempted=0, failed=0`) incorrectly authorized destructive stale-generation fencing;
- exact-digest replenish wrote an immutable runtime ref into `docker_image`, while claims still required the configured tag, making newly-created capacity unclaimable;
- the target digest was absent while a new row was provisioning, so repeated cycles could ignore in-flight capacity and exceed the configured ceiling;
- a retained remote-cleanup tombstone was reported as `replaced`, and daemon deferred arithmetic could count one generation twice;
- floor wording implied claimable capacity even though every known-stale row is deliberately fenced. It now explicitly describes only retained physical teardown capacity.

The repaired contract persists the configured claim image and canonical target digest atomically at pool-row creation, launches Docker from `repo@digest`, preserves the configured image on the ready row, rejects zero-attempt rollout admission, counts in-flight digest generations toward the hard ceiling, verifies row absence before reporting replacement, and keeps all known-stale/null/malformed generations unclaimable.

# Risks

Low. Single-package (`plugin-whatsapp`), one small state flag plus three narrow checks, no public API/signature change (`start()`/`stop()` keep their existing signatures). The fix only ever causes `start()` to return earlier than before (never later, never differently on the non-stopped path) — a session that is never stopped behaves identically to today, confirmed by the added "still restarts normally when stop() is never called" regression test.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`WhatsAppPairingSession` (`plugins/plugin-whatsapp/src/services/whatsapp-pairing.ts`) manages a Baileys QR-pairing session. On a transient disconnect (`restartRequired`/`timedOut`/`connectionClosed`/`connectionReplaced`), its `connection.update` handler schedules a restart: `this.restartTimer = setTimeout(() => { this.restartTimer = null; this.start()... }, 3000)`. `stop()` only cleared `restartTimer` if it hadn't fired yet — it tracked no "was this session stopped" state at all. If `stop()` runs *after* the timer fired but *while* the resulting `start()` call is still mid-flight (awaiting `useMultiFileAuthState`/`fetchLatestBaileysVersion`, neither cancellable), `start()` had no way to know it should abort, and still ran `this.socket = makeWASocket(...)` at the end — resurrecting a live WhatsApp WebSocket connection for a session the caller believes is stopped.

This is reachable from a public route: `POST /api/whatsapp/pair/stop` and `POST /api/whatsapp/disconnect` (`plugins/plugin-whatsapp/src/api/whatsapp-routes.ts`) both call `session.stop()` and then delete the session from their tracking `Map` in the same request — so the caller has no remaining reference to the resurrected session and can never stop it again short of restarting the process. The resurrected socket keeps firing real `connection.update` events into the original `onEvent` closure, which can still transition to `"connected"` — silently re-establishing a WhatsApp pairing after the user explicitly disconnected it.

The sibling class `BaileysConnection` (`plugins/plugin-whatsapp/src/baileys/connection.ts`) already solves this exact shape of race with socket-identity checks (`if (this.socket !== socket) return;`) in every event handler, with its own pinned regression test (`connection.test.ts`, `"ignores a delayed close event from a replaced socket"`). `WhatsAppPairingSession` never received the equivalent treatment. Socket-identity comparison doesn't directly apply here (the race is "stopped while no new socket exists yet", not "stale event from a replaced socket"), so this PR adds a `stopped` boolean instead: set `true` in `stop()`; reset to `false` at the top of `start()` (so a legitimate re-pair still works); checked in two places — right before the socket is created (aborts an in-flight restart, matching the proven repro) and at the top of the `connection.update` handler (so a stale socket's late events, e.g. a final close event Baileys might still emit after `.end()`, can't schedule yet another restart once the session is stopped).

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`plugins/plugin-whatsapp/src/services/whatsapp-pairing.test.ts` (new file) — two tests, mirroring the mocking style already established in `baileys/connection.test.ts`:

1. `"does not resurrect a socket when stop() runs during an in-flight restart"` — pins the exact repro: start a session, trigger a transient close (schedules a 3s restart), advance fake timers to fire it, gate the restart's `fetchLatestBaileysVersion` call open so `start()` is provably mid-flight, call `stop()` while blocked there, then release the gate. Asserts `makeWASocket` is called exactly once (no resurrection).
2. `"still restarts normally when stop() is never called"` — the same transient-close-and-restart flow with no `stop()` call, asserting the restart still happens (`makeWASocket` called twice) — proves the fix doesn't break the legitimate reconnect path it's built on top of.

Confirmed test 1 is a real regression test, not vacuous: reverted the source fix locally and re-ran — it fails with `expected "vi.fn()" to be called 1 times, but got 2 times` (a second socket was created), exactly reproducing the bug. Re-applied the fix and it passes again.

# Risks

Medium. This changes physical-iOS signing and provisioning gates. A profile
that does not grant the checked-in target claims, a bundle with a substituted
or missing appex, or an operational profile-install failure now stops the lane
instead of producing a reduced or falsely signed install.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The squash merge for #21109 retained the base device lane but omitted its
independently reviewed entitlement-integrity corrections. This follow-up:

- validates the app and all five maintained appexes against their checked-in
  entitlement sources and exact bundle identifiers;
- signs only target claims plus the required concrete identity/debug keys,
  rather than copying the provisioning profile's entire allowlist;
- preserves explicit nested-code verification for the app and XCUITest runner;
- uses one target map for deployment and provisioning;
- rejects unsafe ASC profile UUID/id filenames; and
- validates profile bytes in a same-filesystem quarantine before atomic
  publication, while preserving the merged non-destructive replacement-profile
  behavior and surfacing operational install failures.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No additional documentation change is needed. The current app guide already
describes the full-target entitlement and provisioning contract; this PR makes
the implementation match it.

# Risks

Low to medium. The change affects local browser navigation acknowledgements, model-routing truth, embedding-disabled recall behavior, local voice identity, Vite proxy authority, and sparse trajectory rendering. It does not change cloud infrastructure, native macOS/iOS runtime code, secrets, or database schemas.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- lets the model acknowledge successful navigation only after the VIEWS action settles, instead of emitting a hardcoded/premature success string;
- prevents the fallback error reply seen after `go back` when embeddings are intentionally absent;
- preserves browser-selected local API authority and local voice identity;
- renders sparse trajectory model/tool metadata honestly;
- keeps keyword recall useful and quiet when canonical routing omits embeddings;
- skips the disabled embedding warmup instead of generating a false user-visible error.

PR #22288 is already merged in the base. This PR contains only the eight remaining unique browser/runtime commits; it does not duplicate or rewrite the merged overlay work.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking changes).

# Documentation changes needed?

No project documentation change is required. Operator/runtime evidence is attached and the local browser STATUS handoff is updated separately.

The concrete regression flow is: type `open notes`, verify `/notes` opens and receives a model-generated acknowledgement, then type `go back`, verify `/chat` returns and receives a second acknowledgement without a hardcoded `Opened Notes.` response or embedding error.

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"openai","model":"gpt-5.6-sol","client":"Codex Desktop","skill_revision":"elizaOS/eliza@6d068e2ec1c915cf59b1ea93070a26af1ecdec81:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza"} -->


38:T2268,Closes the three findings from my review of #21851 that were still open on `develop` after it merged. I re-verified all three against `d4389a7507c` before writing a line — the fourth finding (the epoch check in `commitAgentBackupRestore`) was genuinely fixed and is not touched here.

Blast radius today is zero: `recordAgentVaultKeySeedReceipt` and `commitAgentBackupRestore` still have no production callers. These fences harden the surface before #20732's later slices consume it.

## 1. The vault-seed writer had no catalogue-epoch fence

`catalog_epoch` was compared in exactly one place on `develop` — `agent-backup-restore-history.ts:711`, inside `commitAgentBackupRestore`. The seed writer locked the backup, lease and sandbox and revalidated operation id, activation generation, lifecycle revision, manifest and expiry, but never loaded `agent_backup_catalog_authorities`.

So a stale-epoch attempt was refused a lease by `acquireAgentBackupRestoreLease` and **still wrote a permanent seed receipt** into a table migration `0250` makes append-only. It can never be removed. Criterion 5 of #20730 asks for revalidation of current mutable authorities *before* appending immutable receipts.

The authority lock goes between the backup and the lease deliberately. Both writers now take the same order — I extracted it from the source rather than assuming:

```
recordAgentVaultKeySeedReceipt   backup -> authority -> lease -> sandbox -> dockerNodes
commitAgentBackupRestore         backup -> authority -> lease -> sandbox -> dockerNodes
```

Any other position for that lock deadlocks the two against each other.

## 2. The finalizer never revalidated the node incarnation

`lockCurrentNodeHistory` was called three times in that file, all before `commitAgentBackupRestore` at `:570`. The finalizer compared `sandbox.activation_boot_id` to `publication.node_incarnation` but never row-locked `docker_nodes`, so a target that rebooted between publication and commit earned a permanent receipt attesting a restore onto an incarnation that no longer exists — while `authorizeAgentActivationDispatch` rejects that exact rotation.

The finalizer now takes the same lock the seed writer takes before its own append-only write.

## 3. The re-arm wedge

`0246:10` kept a global `UNIQUE (node_incarnation)` next to the composite `(docker_node_record_id, node_incarnation)`, and the trigger resolved on the global arbiter (`ON CONFLICT ("node_incarnation") DO NOTHING`, `0246:34`). A boot UUID therefore stayed bound to the first `docker_nodes` row that ever carried it: delete that row, re-register the same host without rebooting, and the journal refused the incarnation forever. Reachable from `api/v1/admin/docker-nodes/[nodeId]/route.ts:310` and the autoscaler.

`0253` drops the global constraint and re-targets the trigger on the composite key, which already carried the invariant that matters — for a given record and incarnation the typed identity is immutable. The global key conflated "boot UUID" with "node record". No foreign key referenced it (`0247:31` and `0248:54` both point at `receipt_authority_unique`).

`lockCurrentNodeHistory` moves to the same composite key in the same commit. That half is not cosmetic: with the global unique gone, reading back on the incarnation alone under `limit(1)` would return an arbitrary record's row.

**Two consequences worth stating rather than burying.**

`0246` is no longer re-runnable once `0253` has landed, because its backfill arbitrates on the constraint `0253` removes (`42P10`). The journal-based migrator never produces that sequence, so the replay proofs apply `0253` after the `0246-0250` stack instead of inside it — every pre-existing assertion is kept, and `0253` gets its own idempotence proof on top.

Dropping the global unique also means two *live* `docker_nodes` rows can now carry the same incarnation, where the trigger previously refused the second. I checked every consumer: the three CAS predicates at `docker-nodes.ts:276,313,418` are all scoped by `eq(dockerNodes.id, input.id)`, receipt foreign keys are composite, and the only remaining single-column read is the one this PR makes composite. No known break — but it is a real loosening, not just an unblocking.

## Verification

Every fix is mutation-proofed. Restores are `git checkout` against the committed object, each mutation asserts its anchor exists before writing, and the baseline is taken before *and* after the series:

```
baseline before                                    authority 9/0   history 11/0
remove the epoch fence                             authority 8/1
remove lockCurrentNodeHistory from the finalizer    authority 8/1
remove DROP CONSTRAINT from 0253                                   history 10/1
baseline after                                     authority 9/0   history 11/0
```

The 0253 tests would not merely fail on the base — the migration file does not exist there, so `readFileSync` throws at module load.

One trap worth flagging for anyone touching migrations here: the PGlite harnesses apply a **hard-coded list**, not the migrations directory. `MIGRATION_NAMES` stopped at `0250`, so a new migration is green-but-never-applied until it is added. `BACKUP_CATALOGUE_MIGRATION_TAGS` additionally pins `idx = 217 + offset` and `when = 1787947200000 + offset * 86_400_000`, which is why `0251` and `0252` are registered here too — they were never added after their own PR, and contiguity would not allow `0253` without them.

**Package typecheck: clean.** Note that it excludes `**/*.test.ts`, so type errors in tests only surface by running them.

**The full `packages/cloud/shared` suite is red on `develop` itself** — 202 failures across CacheClient, OpenRouter, `canonicalWalletAuthQuery`, logger and payouts. I ran the same suite on a detached `origin/develop` and diffed the failing test *names*, not just the count:

```
comm -13 fails-develop.txt fails-branch.txt   -> empty (no regressions)
comm -23 fails-develop.txt fails-branch.txt   -> empty (nothing accidentally fixed)
```

Identical sets. Raw red on that suite proves nothing on its own; the branch-vs-develop name diff is the only signal that does.

## Open question for you

Rotating a host key **in place** — same record, same boot, changed fingerprint — still conflicts after this change, because the fingerprint is part of the attested identity of that incarnation. That is defensible, but it is a real ops wedge until the host reboots. I did not touch it: relaxing it would change what the journal's immutability means, and that is your call, not a decision to slip into a fix PR. Happy to follow up either way.

39:T5b8,## Summary

Follow-up hardening for the bounded remote pairing work merged in #22129.

- bind the pairing verifier to purpose, tenant, user, agent, session, expiry, and code
- authorize ownership on the primary database inside the same transaction that creates, lists, or revokes a session
- serialize concurrent challenge creation so only one pending challenge remains per agent
- make concurrent revocation atomic and idempotent
- validate agent UUIDs and keep client-supplied authority/redirect fields out of the verifier context

This remains intentionally scoped to challenge issuance/list/revoke. No consume or device-grant endpoint exists yet, so this does not claim end-to-end production pairing.

# Risks

Medium, because CLI-login session identifiers are unauthenticated handoff capabilities. Every create caller now sends a fresh cryptographic v4 compatibility proposal for the currently deployed API, then validates and exclusively trusts the server-returned UUID. Missing/malformed responses fail closed before browser navigation or polling, and capability-bearing URLs are not logged.

SSO minting remains fail-closed. Managed-Cloud startup now cancels and drains the losing readiness probe.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This addresses the three actionable follow-up findings from #22082 and repairs every caller of the shared login-session contract:

1. **Authoritative server session identity:** UI native/web, `@elizaos/cloud-sdk`, plugin setup login, the plugin's autonomous local route, and the zero-import test console validate and use only the response UUID. They send a fresh cryptographic UUID proposal solely for compatibility with the currently deployed endpoint; there is no response fallback.
2. **Referrer-stripped SSO handoff:** an absent referrer still mints nothing, but redirects to the exact paired app origin's normal `/login` with sanitized `returnTo`. Explicit cross-site referrers mint nothing and receive no paired-origin redirect.
3. **Immediate auth gate with cancellation:** a definitive `auth-required` conversations response aborts and drains the concurrent 30-second status probe before mounting the auth gate. A decisive status result likewise aborts and drains the conversations probe.
4. **Capability-safe diagnostics:** the plugin no longer logs the browser handoff URL/session capability; regression coverage asserts the authoritative UUID is absent from logger calls.

The runtime-target Settings behavior noted in #22082 is intentionally unchanged because it is product behavior, not this defect.

## What kind of change is this?

Security-sensitive bug fixes with regression coverage. The deprecated SDK `sessionId` option remains source-compatible but is ignored.

# Documentation changes needed?

No user-facing documentation change. The implementation aligns clients with both the checked-in hardened server contract and the currently deployed compatibility requirement.

## Outcome

- packages the exact Linux Electrobun build tree as a side-loadable Flatpak alongside AppImage, DEB, and RPM
- installs the produced bundle and requires a 45-second launch-liveness gate
- makes the existing packaged-desktop Playwright regression lane mandatory on Linux releases
- refuses the retired CLI-based `build:flatpak:store` path instead of presenting it as Flathub-ready
- documents the remaining Flathub offline-source, app-ID verification, metadata, human-submission, and policy-exception blockers

## Why

The previous manifests installed `npm install -g elizaos` and launched a terminal CLI. They did not package the Electrobun desktop that users receive on Linux, and the store manifest requested network access during the build, which Flathub does not permit.

This PR makes the canonical release artifact truthful and testable without claiming the separate Flathub submission is eligible.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds closed, typed `evidenceClass` and `certificationClass` scenario metadata.
- Keeps ordinary legacy definitions compatible as simulated evidence with no certification claim; explicitly provider-qualified definitions retain their provider-observed default.
- Fails closed when deterministic or simulated definitions claim provider, native/device, or webhook certification, or when the certification and evidence boundaries disagree.
- Makes static corpus validation reject non-literal critical metadata, object spreads, and computed fields that could conceal certification claims without importing scenario modules.
- Classifies all 32 current connector-certification scenarios honestly as `runtime-observed` / `runtime-contract`; these labels do not claim provider certification.
- Documents the authoring and static-validation contract in the package README and byte-identical guides.

Explicitly out of scope: #14550 evidence-bundle/reviewer plumbing, #15887 live-information evaluation, #16972 voice/device workbench evidence, provider deployment/credentials, scenario semantic repairs, and external qualification artifacts.

Testing-contract hardening and truthful corpus metadata.

## What changed

A synchronous WebSocket send count is not a renderer acknowledgement. This change gives every completed VIEWS handoff a bounded, validated id carried through the targeted frame and terminal result, then deduplicates only from state observed by the originating renderer.

- A mounted shell acknowledges a handled cancelable navigation event.
- An unhandled first delivery remains retryable while the renderer stays on the same route epoch.
- Explicit intervening navigation wins: if the path or popstate/hashchange epoch advances before fallback, the pending handoff is retired and cannot pull the user back.
- The same rule covers WebSocket-first and terminal-first orderings.
- Handled and pending histories are bounded to 256 entries.
- Legacy results without an id keep their existing behavior; zero-recipient/send-throw fallback, older servers, client targeting, and realtime voice remain unchanged.

## Deterministic transport transcript

```text
Handled WebSocket-first and terminal-first handoffs navigate once; after the user moves to /settings, their duplicate delivery is suppressed and /settings remains active.
An unhandled early frame retries when the route epoch is unchanged, but a changed path before terminal fallback retires the id without dispatching into the mounted shell.
A same-path popstate epoch advance also retires the pending id, preventing history-state user navigation from being overwritten by a late transport fallback.
```

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto live `develop@0bf4826f0026b81e8edfb9d23f470f644254f298`.
- [x] Exact head: `c60b0175416d68d9df681e5916f8dc3dd1f47940`.
- [x] Exactly one repair commit atop `origin/develop` (4 files, +397 -47).

# Risks

Low. Adds strict timeouts and streaming byte limits to two Discord avatar download paths. Remote fetch failures degrade gracefully (cache path returns original volatile URL, profile sync throws a typed size error the caller already handles). No new network surface, no schema or API change.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds `DISCORD_AVATAR_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS=15s` / `MAX_DISCORD_AVATAR_BYTES=2MiB` (cache) and `PROFILE_AVATAR_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS=15s` / `MAX_PROFILE_AVATAR_BYTES=8MiB` (profile sync).
- Enforces early `Content-Length` rejection when the declared length exceeds the limit.
- Replaces unbounded `response.arrayBuffer()` buffering with streaming `ReadableStream.getReader()` that cancels the stream immediately when cumulative bytes exceed the limit (`reader.cancel()` + `J6` teardown, `reader.releaseLock()`).
- Validates `Content-Type: image/*` and host allowlist (`cdn.discordapp.com`, `media.discordapp.net`, `images-ext-1/2.discordapp.net`) before caching.
- Preserves existing cache-hit/inflight-dedup and atomic `writeFile+rename` behavior.

## What kind of change is this?

Resource-exhaustion hardening with no public API change.

# Documentation changes needed?

No documentation change is needed; timeout/byte-limit behavior is internal hardening.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`plugins/plugin-discord/__tests__/discord-avatar-cache.test.ts` (host allowlist, Content-Length rejection, 5×1 MiB chunked bomb, valid PNG cache-hit) and `plugins/plugin-discord/__tests__/profile-sync-avatar-diagnostics.test.ts` (oversized remote avatar, streaming bomb).

# Risks

Low. Adds a 10s `AbortSignal.timeout` to the single Kubernetes API `fetch` in each gateway's `resolvePodIPs`. Timeout or network failure degrades to `[]` (empty hash ring) via existing `try/catch` + `logger` path — no throw, no crash, no new API surface or schema change.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds `ENDPOINT_SLICE_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS=10_000` in:
  - `packages/cloud/services/gateway-discord/src/hash-router.ts`
  - `packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook/src/hash-router.ts`
- Passes `signal: AbortSignal.timeout(ENDPOINT_SLICE_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS)` to `fetch(apiUrl, { headers, tls, signal })` during EndpointSlice discovery.
- Without this, a stalled or hung `kubernetes.default.svc` connection wedges gateway hash-ring refresh and blocks request routing.

## What kind of change is this?

Liveness hardening with no public API change.

# Documentation changes needed?

No documentation change is needed; timeout behavior is internal gateway liveness hardening.

# Testing

# Risks

Low. The change is confined to `plugins/plugin-native-camera/src/web.ts` (the browser `CameraWeb` fallback). It adds explicit teardown of a microphone stream that was previously leaked; it does not alter the recorder output, the returned `VideoResult`, or any public API. The new release path is null-guarded and idempotent, so it cannot double-stop tracks or affect audio-disabled recordings.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Fixes a microphone resource/privacy leak in `CameraWeb`. `startRecording({ audio: true })` acquires a **separate** microphone `MediaStream` via `getUserMedia({ audio: true })` and splices its audio tracks into the combined recorder stream, but never retained a reference to that stream. `stopRecording()` only called `mediaRecorder.stop()` (which does not stop underlying tracks) and `stopPreview()` only stopped `this.mediaStream` (the camera stream — video tracks only). The mic tracks lived in neither, so the microphone stayed live after a recording session ended and even after the preview was torn down. On real browsers/Electron this keeps the OS microphone indicator lit and the mic hardware engaged indefinitely, and each subsequent record cycle acquired and leaked another mic track.

The fix:
- Retains the recording mic stream on the instance (`this.recordingAudioStream`) when acquired in `startRecording`.
- Stops its tracks in the `stopRecording` `onstop` handler via a null-guarded, idempotent `releaseRecordingAudio()` helper.
- Adds a defensive `releaseRecordingAudio()` call in `stopPreview()` for the case where preview teardown interrupts an in-flight recording.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. The package `CLAUDE.md`/`README.md` describe `stopRecording`/`stopPreview` as releasing camera resources; correctly releasing the microphone is the documented-but-previously-broken behavior, not a new capability.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`plugins/plugin-native-camera/src/web.test.ts` — the new `describe("CameraWeb recording lifecycle releases the microphone", ...)` block, and the three-line teardown addition in `plugins/plugin-native-camera/src/web.ts`.

## Detailed testing steps

```bash
bun install
cd plugins/plugin-native-camera
../../node_modules/.bin/vitest run src/web.test.ts
bun run typecheck
bun run lint:check
```

Automated tests are sufficient: the regression tests spy on the mocked mic track's `stop()` and assert it is invoked exactly once on teardown, never re-stopped, and released across a second record cycle and on `stopPreview` interrupting an active recording.

# Risks

Medium. Worker file reads and writes now require a canonical component walk and
descriptor identity validation. Portable Node lacks `openat`/`mkdirat`; a
hostile concurrent intermediate rename can at worst leave an empty file or
directory, but cannot pass content I/O validation.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Walks from the canonical sandbox root and rejects symlink or non-directory
  path components.
- Creates missing write directories one component at a time, revalidating each.
- Opens the final target with `O_NOFOLLOW` where supported.
- Requires the opened descriptor and path to match by device/inode, then checks
  the final canonical path before any read, truncate, or write.
- Marshals stable worker error code/context into a host-side `ElizaError`.
- Covers nested symlink escapes and descriptor swap/restore behavior through the
  real AppWorkerHost boundary.

## What kind of change is this?

Security bug fix; no intended public API change.

# Documentation changes needed?

No. This enforces the existing worker state-directory boundary.
